Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate it. 

Um, I hope I'm not taking time away from people who need it more, but I just... well, I've been having this sort of constant restless feeling. It's probably just normal stress, I mean, I manage fine overall, but I just feel like I'm wound a bit too tight, and I can't seem to fully settle.

Oh, um... that's a really good question. I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, but... honestly, I don't think I'm really worrying about anything?

Oh, um... no, I don't think it's every single day. I really hope that doesn't make my being here seem silly, but I definitely don't want to exaggerate anything.

It's just that I can't always sit still to actually do them, if that makes sense? But the interest is absolutely still there. I really try to keep a positive outlook on life, so I don't think I have any problems with feeling down. \n\nIs that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where your heart races, you feel short of breath, or you feel like something terrible is going to happen — even when there's no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that at all. \n\nI mean, I hope I'm understanding you correctly, but... I've never felt like I couldn't breathe or that something terrible was going to happen. That sounds really frightening, actually! \n\nMy heart might beat a little fast if I'm rushing around trying to get a purchase order out on time, but that's just normal work stuff, you know? It's never out of nowhere, and it's never scary like that. I really just have that... that fidgety, restless feeling in my body. \n\nIs it okay that I don't have those symptoms? I hope I'm not wasting your time since it's just the restlessness.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed this restless feeling happening nearly every day for at least several weeks, and does it occur even when things in your life are going smoothly without any particular stressors?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think it's every single day. Oh, dear... I think this is a lot like what you asked just a moment ago, isn't it? I feel just awful having to give you the exact same answer again! But no, I really don't find myself worrying about a bunch of different things.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, yes, I suppose it does happen when I'm alone, too. But I really don't think it's anything to worry about! It's just a little bit of excess energy. 

Like, when I'm working from home and I'm on calls with my suppliers... I guess I do tend to bounce my foot under the desk quite a bit. And I might be clicking a pen or twisting my ring around. Sometimes I actually have to mute the microphone or kind of hide my hands off-camera so it doesn't distract anyone. 

But I always make sure it doesn't bother anyone else! I'm very careful about that. It's just a little habit to help me focus, you know? It's not like I'm jumping out of my chair or anything disruptive. I hope that doesn't sound too bad? I just want to be completely honest with you, even about the silly little habits I have. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, maybe a little bit? But I really wouldn't call it being "agitated"—I definitely don't want you to think I'm running around causing a scene or being rude! 

But, I suppose... if a meeting at work runs really long, or, oh goodness, if we're having one of those tense family dinners with my in-laws... I do have a hard time just staying in my chair. I try to be really polite about it, though! I'll just keep finding little reasons to get up. Like, I'll offer to fetch everyone some water, or go check the oven, or if I'm at the office, I'll pop up to look at some new vendor samples instead of just sitting there. 

I just sort of... prefer to be up and doing things rather than sitting glued to a seat for hours on end. It's really just me trying to be helpful and productive, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too... difficult or hyper? I just like to make myself useful to the group. Is that the kind of thing you meant?

And I wouldn't say I get fatigued... I actually have quite a bit of energy, maybe a little too much sometimes, which is why I'm always up and doing things. And my muscles feel fine, no real tension or anything like that. \n\nI just... I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers. I know I'm saying 'no' to a lot of things, and I really hope I'm not being difficult or unhelpful. Is it alright that I don't have those issues?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a number of different things, even when you don't have an obvious reason to be concerned, or does your restlessness seem to be more connected to specific stressful events?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm a worrier. I really hope that's a good thing! I mean, I don't sit around worrying about all sorts of different things for no reason. \n\nIf I'm stressed about a big shipment being delayed, well, sure, I might feel a little antsy about that specific thing, but that's just because I want to do a good job for my clients. It's not this big, generalized worry cloud hanging over me. Most of the time, my mind is pretty clear, even if my body feels like it needs to be moving. \n\nI just... I hope I'm making sense. I know I keep saying no to your questions, and I really don't want you to think I'm wasting your time. Is it okay that I'm not a big worrier?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you can’t control the restless feeling, even when you want to, or does it only happen during certain activities or situations?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no... I definitely wouldn't say I can't control it. I really hope that doesn't ruin whatever you're trying to figure out! But no, if I need to sit still—like for a long vendor presentation or a budget meeting—I absolutely can. \n\nI mean, I might prefer to be up and doing things, but I'm always in control of it. It's not like it just takes over my body or anything scary like that. It's mostly just when I have a little downtime that I feel the urge to get moving again. \n\nI hope it's okay that I can manage it. I just want to be as helpful as possible, and I know I'm probably not giving you the textbook answers you might be looking for. I'd hate for you to think I'm not cooperating. Is it alright that I feel like I still have a pretty good handle on it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling anxious or tense most days, even when nothing specific is bothering you, like a background hum of worry or unease?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no... I don't think so. I really don't feel anxious like that. I feel terrible, I know I must sound like a broken record just telling you 'no' over and over! \n\nBut honestly, I don't have that background hum of unease you're talking about. I'm generally a pretty upbeat person. I like to keep busy, you know, checking on my vendors and staying on top of the seasonal orders, but it's definitely not because I'm tense or secretly worried about anything. \n\nI just... I hope I'm not being a difficult patient by not having all these issues. I really want to give you what you need to do your job. Is it okay that I just feel pretty, well, normal in that regard? I hope I'm not frustrating you.\n\nDoctor: Has this restlessness ever made it hard for you to do your job, interact with others, or caused problems in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say it's caused any *major* problems. I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly! But, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, sometimes at work I can get a little ahead of myself. It’s led to a few corrected purchase orders here and there, and my planning coordinator has started doing some extra review on my files. Which is totally fine, she’s just being helpful! I really appreciate her catching things when I'm moving a bit too fast. \n\nAnd, um, at home... I guess family dinners can get just a tiny bit tense sometimes. I don't mean to be rude at all, but I just get so energized and jump into the conversation, and my family has mentioned that they feel a little talked over. I feel awful about it, I really do. I just get so eager to share and keep the conversation going! \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible coworker or a bad listener. I really do try to be considerate of everyone's feelings. Is that... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about a number of different things more days than not, even if you can’t quite put your finger on why?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I think this is a lot like what you asked just a moment ago, isn't it? I feel just awful having to give you the exact same answer again! But no, I really don't find myself worrying about a bunch of different things. \n\nI mean, I care about my job and my family, obviously, and I want things to go well with my seasonal buys... but I don't sit around feeling anxious or worried for no reason. I'm actually a pretty optimistic person overall! If a shipment gets delayed, I just tackle it, I don't really agonize over it beforehand. \n\nI hope it's not too frustrating for you that I keep saying no to these worry questions. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the absolute truth so you can do your assessment properly. Am I still giving you enough to work with? I'd hate to think I'm wasting your time by not having more to report.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt irritable or had trouble sitting still for reasons that were hard to explain, even when things were going well in your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I feel like I'm failing a test here by saying no again! But honestly, no, I don't really get irritable like that. I always try my hardest to look on the bright side and be as pleasant as possible with my team and my family. I'd hate to be the kind of person who just snaps at people for no reason! \n\nAs for the sitting still part... I mean, I know I mentioned earlier that I like to be on the move and I get very energized, but it doesn't feel like some strange, uncomfortable restlessness that I can't explain. It's just... well, I just have a lot of pep! I like being active and staying on top of things. It's not a bad feeling at all. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have that kind of unexplained irritability to report. I really do want to be helpful... is this still useful for your notes? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job answering your questions.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel unusually anxious or self-conscious in situations where others might be watching you, like during a presentation at work or even just speaking up in a group setting, even if there's no real reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I am so sorry, I feel like a broken record today! But no, I really don't get anxious about people watching me or speaking up. Actually, I think I’m quite the opposite! \n\nIn my line of work, dealing with vendors and presenting the seasonal buys to the team, you really have to be comfortable talking in front of groups. I really don't feel self-conscious or worried about people judging me in those settings. \n\nI hope it’s alright that I keep saying no to these anxiety questions! I know you have a specific list you need to go through. I just want to make sure I'm painting an accurate picture for you so you can do your job. Am I still doing okay with all this?\n\nDoctor: So, just to be sure I understand clearly, when you feel this restless or fidgety, does it ever happen even when you’re completely alone and there’s no pressure or expectations from others, like at home on a quiet evening?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, yes, I suppose it does happen when I'm alone, too. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel pressure to perform perfectly or become overly concerned about making mistakes, even in routine tasks?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I'm overly concerned about making mistakes. I'd never want to be one of those people who gets so stressed out that it brings the whole team down. \n\nI hope that's okay to say? I just try to do my best and keep things moving. I really hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... I feel like I keep saying no to these things, and I don't want to be difficult!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when you felt so restless or physically active that others noticed you were unusually agitated or unable to sit still, even if you weren’t feeling anxious or stressed about anything?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, maybe a little bit? Like, I'll offer to fetch everyone some water, or go check the oven, or if I'm at the office, I'll pop up to look at some new vendor samples instead of just sitting there. \n\nI just sort of... prefer to be up and doing things rather than sitting glued to a seat for hours on end. It's really just me trying to be helpful and productive, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too... difficult or hyper? I just like to make myself useful to the group. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel distressed or concerned about this restless feeling, even if you're not exactly sure why it happens?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I feel distressed about it at all! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... I think it's definitely more of a physical need to move. I really wouldn't call it nervous or tense, because I don't want you to think I'm stressed out or anxious! I'm actually a very positive person. 

But it's just... well, honestly, it's sort of what brought me in to see you. I just have this constant restless feeling, almost like I'm... wound a little too tight? Like my body just can't fully settle, even when I really want it to. Like if I'm supposed to be calmly sitting through a long planning meeting with the vendors, or even on my own time when I'm painting or trying to do yoga... my body just sort of hums and wants to get going. 

I try so hard to just relax and be perfectly still during those times, I really do! I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so I don't sound like a nervous wreck or anything. Is that the kind of description you were looking for?
